I don't really second-guess if I can handle it, I just feel tense while I'm actually in the middle of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel tense or overwhelmed when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Um, once I clock out and leave the warehouse, that tense feeling pretty much goes away. I don't sit around at home feeling overwhelmed... I'm mostly just exhausted, like I said before. So, yeah, the tension is really just while I'm actually on the clock.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any physical symptoms like headaches, stomach issues, or tremors when you're at work?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I don't get headaches or stomachaches or anything. Just the feeling of being tense and tired, like I mentioned. But no physical stuff like you're asking about.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: No, my sleep has been fine. Like I said, I'm pretty exhausted by the time I get home, so I usually just crash. I haven't had any trouble falling asleep or staying asleep.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your relationships with friends or family since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess with my friends, I've stopped showing up for basketball. It just feels like work is taking over my life right now. I'm not even doing my usual sketching time at home anymore. \n\nAnd honestly, it's affecting the job itself, too. My supervisor actually had to bring up some missed cycle-count deadlines during a recent performance check-in. So, yeah, I'm just kind of dropping the ball on a lot of regular things.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your relationships with family members since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Uh, no, not really. I haven't noticed any changes with my family. We still talk the same amount and everything seems fine on that end. It's mostly just been backing out of things with my friends and the work stuff I mentioned. But with family, no, things are normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're withdrawing from social activities in general, not just basketball and sketching?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, sketching is just something I do by myself anyway, so that's not really social. And besides basketball, I don't have a ton of other group activities going on during a regular week. So it's not like I'm hiding from everyone or anything like that... I just don't have the energy to go out and do the few things I normally would.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your tension and exhaustion are significantly impacting your overall quality of life?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to answer that. I haven't really thought about it in terms of my... overall quality of life, I guess. It's more just that the day-to-day stuff is a lot harder right now. \n\nLike I mentioned, I'm just too drained for basketball, and the warehouse deadlines are stressing me out. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I'm here because things have been pretty rough at work lately. A little while ago, they made me the acting workflow lead at the warehouse. Ever since then, I've just been... well, I walk in the door already tense. I find myself snapping at people over minor SAP errors, which isn't like me. And I'm missing cycle-count deadlines that I never used to miss. By the time my shift is over, I'm just completely drained. I don't even have the energy to play basketball or work on my sketches when I get home anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad most of the time since you became the acting workflow lead?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't really say I've been feeling sad. It's more just... I'm stressed out and overwhelmed by all the new responsibilities. I'm just tense and drained, but not really sad or down.\n\nDoctor: Do you often worry about not being able to handle your new responsibilities as acting workflow lead?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I don't sit around worrying that I'm going to fail or that I can't do the job. I know the warehouse pretty well. It's more just... the actual day-to-day pressure of getting it all done is what's burning me out. I don't really second-guess if I can handle it, I just feel tense while I'm actually in the middle of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel tense or overwhelmed when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Um, once I clock out and leave the warehouse, that tense feeling pretty much goes away. I don't sit around at home feeling overwhelmed... I'm mostly just exhausted, like I said before. So, yeah, the tension is really just while I'm actually on the clock.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any physical symptoms like headaches, stomach issues, or tremors when you're at work?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I don't get headaches or stomachaches or anything. Just the feeling of being tense and tired, like I mentioned. But no physical stuff like you're asking about.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: No, my sleep has been fine. Like I said, I'm pretty exhausted by the time I get home, so I usually just crash. I haven't had any trouble falling asleep or staying asleep.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your relationships with friends or family since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess with my friends, I've stopped showing up for basketball. It just feels like work is taking over my life right now. I'm not even doing my usual sketching time at home anymore. \n\nAnd honestly, it's affecting the job itself, too. My supervisor actually had to bring up some missed cycle-count deadlines during a recent performance check-in. So, yeah, I'm just kind of dropping the ball on a lot of regular things.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your relationships with family members since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Uh, no, not really. I haven't noticed any changes with my family. We still talk the same amount and everything seems fine on that end. It's mostly just been backing out of things with my friends and the work stuff I mentioned. But with family, no, things are normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're withdrawing from social activities in general, not just basketball and sketching?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, sketching is just something I do by myself anyway, so that's not really social. And besides basketball, I don't have a ton of other group activities going on during a regular week. So it's not like I'm hiding from everyone or anything like that... I just don't have the energy to go out and do the few things I normally would.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your tension and exhaustion are significantly impacting your overall quality of life?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to answer that. I haven't really thought about it in terms of my... overall quality of life, I guess. It's more just that the day-to-day stuff is a lot harder right now. \n\nLike I mentioned, I'm just too drained for basketball, and the warehouse deadlines are stressing me out. So it definitely sucks and it's frustrating, but I don't know if I'd look at it like this big-picture thing. I'm just really tired of feeling this way every day.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling tense and overwhelmed at work?\n\nPatient: It's been about four months now. That's when they merged the inventory teams at the warehouse and, uh, put me in the acting workflow lead role. So yeah, basically since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having persistent thoughts or concerns about work when you're not there?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, when I clock out of the warehouse, I'm pretty much done with it for the day. I'm definitely exhausted when I get home, but I'm not sitting around obsessing over inventory or deadlines or anything like that. So, yeah, the tension is really just while I'm actually on the clock.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any physical symptoms like headaches, stomach issues, or tremors when you're at work?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I don't get headaches or stomachaches or anything. Just the feeling of being tense and tired, like I mentioned. But no physical stuff like you're asking about.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: No, my sleep has been fine. Like I said, I'm pretty exhausted by the time I get home, so I usually just crash. I haven't had any trouble falling asleep or staying asleep.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your relationships with friends or family since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess with my friends, I've stopped showing up for basketball. It just feels like work is taking over my life right now. I'm not even doing my usual sketching time at home anymore. \n\nAnd honestly, it's affecting the job itself, too. My supervisor actually had to bring up some missed cycle-count deadlines during a recent performance check-in. So, yeah, I'm just kind of dropping the ball on a lot of regular things.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your relationships with family members since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Uh, no, not really. I haven't noticed any changes with my family. We still talk the same amount and everything seems fine on that end. It's mostly just been backing out of things with my friends and the work stuff I mentioned. But with family, no, things are normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're withdrawing from social activities in general, not just basketball and sketching?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, sketching is just something I do by myself anyway, so that's not really social. And besides basketball, I don't have a ton of other group activities going on during a regular week. So it's not like I'm hiding from everyone or anything like that... I just don't have the energy to go out and do the few things I normally would.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your tension and exhaustion are significantly impacting your overall quality of life?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to answer that. I haven't really thought about it in terms of my... overall quality of life, I guess. It's more just that the day-to-day stuff is a lot harder right now. \n\nLike I mentioned, I'm just too drained for basketball, and the warehouse deadlines are stressing me out. So it definitely sucks and it's frustrating, but I don't know if I'd look at it like this big-picture thing. I'm just really tired of feeling this way every day.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling tense and overwhelmed at work?\n\nPatient: It's been about four months now. That's when they merged the inventory teams at the warehouse and, uh, put me in the acting workflow lead role. So yeah, basically since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having persistent thoughts or concerns about work when you're not there?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, when I clock out of the warehouse, I'm pretty much done with it for the day. I'm definitely exhausted when I get home, but I'm not sitting around obsessing over inventory or deadlines or anything like that. I mostly just try to turn my brain off once I'm out the door.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life where you felt similarly tense and overwhelmed, but it wasn't related to work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I've had stressful things happen, sure, but... nothing that made me feel this tense and drained for months on end. It's usually just normal stuff that passes. I've never really felt this overwhelmed until all these changes at the warehouse started.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any anxiety attacks or panic episodes at work or outside of work?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I mean, I feel really stressed and tense, obviously, but... I haven't had an actual panic attack or anything. I don't get out of breath or feel like my heart is racing out of nowhere. It's really just that constant, heavy pressure while I'm on the clock, but no panic episodes.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. And I'm missing cycle-count deadlines that I never used to miss. By the time my shift is over, I'm just completely drained. I don't even have the energy to play basketball or work on my sketches when I get home anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad most of the time since you became the acting workflow lead?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't really say I've been feeling sad. It's more just... I'm stressed out and overwhelmed by all the new responsibilities. I mean, I don't sit around worrying that I'm going to fail or that I can't do the job. I know the warehouse pretty well. It's more just... the actual day-to-day pressure of getting it all done is what's burning me out. I don't really second-guess if I can handle it, I just feel tense while I'm actually in the middle of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel tense or overwhelmed when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I said, I'm pretty exhausted by the time I get home, so I usually just crash. I haven't really thought about it in terms of my... overall quality of life, I guess. I'm just really tired of feeling this way every day.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling tense and overwhelmed at work?\n\nPatient: It's been about four months now. I just feel really stressed out while I'm doing them.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your tension and stress are directly triggered by specific events or tasks at work?\n\nPatient: Not really.
